\displaystyle\leq\bigg(D-\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}\mathbf{S}_{j^{*},j^{\prime}}^{(i)}\bigg)\big\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\big\|_{2}\big\|\mathbf{z}_{j^{*}}^{(i)}\big\|_{2}+\sum_{j\neq j^{*}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{D}\mathbf{S}_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(i)}\big\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\big\|_{2}\big\|\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(i)}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\big(\sqrt{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{x}+1\big)\big(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{D}\big)D\log n}{\gamma e^{C_{11}D}}\leq\frac{1}{\gamma e^{C_{12}D}},

where the penultimate inequality is by Lemma
C.2
, Lemma
D.17
, Lemma
D.18
and definition of
\widehat{\mathbf{v}}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Based on the preceding Lemmas, we are now ready to prove Theorem
4.2
.

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

By updating rule (
4.1
), we can obtain that

\displaystyle\big\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i+1)}-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)}-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\big\|_{2}^{2}+2\big\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i+1)}-\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)}-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\big\rangle+\big\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i+1)}-\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)}\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)}-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\big\|_{2}^{2}-2\widetilde{\eta}\big\langle\nabla_{\mathbf{v}}\mathcal{L}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)}),\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)}-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\big\rangle+\widetilde{\eta}^{2}\big\|\nabla_{\mathbf{v}}\mathcal{L}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)}-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\big\|_{2}^{2}+2\widetilde{\eta}\bigg(\mathcal{L}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{W}})-\mathcal{L}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})+\frac{1}{\gamma e^{C_{12}D}}\bigg)+\widetilde{\eta}^{2}\big\|\nabla_{\mathbf{v}}\mathcal{L}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)}-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\big\|_{2}^{2}+2\widetilde{\eta}\bigg(\mathcal{L}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{W}})-\mathcal{L}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})+\frac{1}{\gamma e^{C_{12}D}}\bigg)+\widetilde{\eta}\mathcal{L}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)}-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\big\|_{2}^{2}-\widetilde{\eta}\mathcal{L}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})+\frac{2\widetilde{\eta}}{\gamma e^{C_{12}D}}+\frac{2\widetilde{\eta}}{n}.

(C.4)

The first inequality holds by Lemma
C.3
. The second inequality holds because

\displaystyle\widetilde{\eta}^{2}\big\|\nabla_{\mathbf{v}}\mathcal{L}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\eta}^{2}\Big(\ell^{\prime}\big(y^{(i)}f(\mathbf{Z}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\big)\Big)^{2}\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{D}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{D}\mathbf{S}_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(i)}\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(i)}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{\eta}^{2}\ell\big(y^{(i)}f(\mathbf{Z}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\big)\big(\sqrt{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{x}+1\big)^{2}\big(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{D}\big)^{2}D^{2}\leq\widetilde{\eta}\mathcal{L}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)}),

where the first inequality holds by the facts that
-\ell^{\prime}(x)\leq\ell(x)
,
-\ell^{\prime}(x)\leq 1
and
\big\|\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(i)}\big\|_{2}\leq\big(\sqrt{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{x}+1\big)\big(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{D}\big)
, and the last inequality holds by our condition that
\widetilde{\eta}\leq\frac{1}{(\sqrt{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{x}+1)^{2}(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{D})^{2}D^{2}}
. And the third inequality is because

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{W}})=\ell\big(y^{(i)}f(\mathbf{Z}^{(i)},\widehat{\mathbf{W}},\widehat{\mathbf{v}})\big)=\ell\bigg(\frac{\log n}{\gamma}y^{(i)}\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}^{(i)}\rangle\bigg)\leq\ell(\log n)\leq\frac{1}{n}.

By rearranging and take a telescoping sum on both side of (
C.4
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{L}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\leq\frac{\big\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(1)}-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{n\widetilde{\eta}}+\frac{2}{\gamma e^{C_{12}D}}+\frac{2}{n}\leq\frac{2(\sqrt{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{x}+1)^{2}\log^{2}n(d+D)}{\gamma^{2}n}+\frac{2}{\gamma e^{C_{12}D}}+\frac{2}{n}.

Further by applying the fact that
\gamma\leq\min_{i\in[n]}\|\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}^{(i)}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{x}\big(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{D}\big)
and
-\ell(x)\leq\ell(x)
, we can get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{R}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{L}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\leq\frac{4(\sqrt{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{x}+1)^{2}\log^{2}n(d+D)}{\gamma^{2}n}+\frac{2}{\gamma e^{C_{12}D}}.

(C.5)

Notice that the quantity
\sum_{i\leq k}\big(\mathcal{R}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})-\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\big)
forms a martingale w.r.t. the filtration
\sigma\Big(\big(\mathbf{Z}^{(1)},y^{(1)}\big),\cdots,\big(\mathbf{Z}^{(k-1)},y^{(k-1)}\big)\Big)=\sigma^{(k)}
. The martingale difference
\mathcal{R}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})-\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})
is bounded by
1
since
-\ell^{\prime}
is bounded. And we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathcal{R}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})-\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\big)^{2}\Big|\sigma^{(i)}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{R}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})^{2}\Big|\sigma^{(i)}\big]-2\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{R}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\Big|\sigma^{(i)}\big]+\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{R}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})^{2}\Big|\sigma^{(i)}\big]-\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{R}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\Big|\sigma^{(i)}\big]-\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})^{2}\leq\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)}).

Therefore by Theorem 1 in
Beygelzimer et al. (2011)
(a version of martingale difference concentration inequality), with probability at least
1-\delta
for any positive
\delta
, it holds that,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})-\mathcal{R}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e-2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathcal{R}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})-\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\big)^{2}\Big|\sigma^{(i)}\Big]+\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e-2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})+\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}.

Rearranging on both sides and applying the results of (
C.5
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{R}_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})+\frac{4\log(1/\delta)}{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16(\sqrt{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{x}+1)^{2}\log^{2}n(d+D)}{\gamma^{2}n}+\frac{4\log(1/\delta)}{n}+\frac{8}{\gamma e^{C_{12}D}}.

Since
\mathbb{P}_{(\mathbf{X},y)\sim\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}}\big(y\cdot f(\mathbf{Z},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\leq 0\big)\leq 2\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{W}},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}})
holds for any
\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}
, we finally derive that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{(\mathbf{X},y)\sim\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}}\Big(y\cdot f(\mathbf{Z},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)})\leq 0\Big)\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}^{(i)})\leq O\bigg(\frac{\log^{2}n(d+D)}{\gamma^{2}n}\bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\bigg),

which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix D
Technical lemmas

D.1
Independence among Gaussian random vectors

Lemma D.1
.

Let
z_{1},z_{2}
be two Gaussian random variables with zero mean, and
y=\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(z_{1})
. Then
y
is independent with
y\cdot z_{1}
and
y\cdot z_{2}
. Moreover,
y\cdot z_{2}
also follows the normal distribution, which has zero mean and the same variance with
z_{2}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.1
.

W.L.O.G, we assume that
z_{1},z_{2}
are standard Gaussian random variables. We first prove that
y
is independent with
y\cdot z_{1}
. It is clear that
y\cdot z_{1}=|z_{1}|
. Then for any
x\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(y\cdot z_{1}\leq x)=\mathbb{P}(|z_{1}|\leq x)=\mathbb{P}(|z_{1}|\leq x|y),

which indicate that
y\cdot z_{1}
has the same distribution with
y\cdot z_{1}|y
. Therefore,
y
is independent with
y\cdot z_{1}
. Next, we prove that
y
is independent with
y\cdot z_{2}
and
y\cdot z_{2}
follows the standard normal distribution. We denote
\Phi(\cdot)
the cumulative density function (c.d.f.) of the standard normal distribution. Then for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(y\cdot z_{2}\leq x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(z_{2}\leq x;y=1)+\mathbb{P}(z_{2}\geq-x;y=-1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(z_{2}\leq x)\cdot\mathbb{P}(y=1)+\mathbb{P}(z_{2}\geq-x)\cdot\mathbb{P}(y=-1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Phi(x)+\frac{1}{2}\big(1-\Phi(-x)\big)=\Phi(x),

where the second equality holds by the independence between
y
and
z_{2}
, and the last equality holds by the symmetry of standard normal distribution that
\Phi(-x)=1-\Phi(x)
. This proves that
y\cdot z_{2}\sim N(0,1)
. Moreover, it is obvious that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(y\cdot z_{2}\leq x|y=1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(z_{2}\leq x|y=1)=\Phi(x),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(y\cdot z_{2}\leq x|y=-1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(z_{2}\geq-x|y=1)=1-\Phi(-x)=\Phi(x),

which indicate that
y\cdot z_{2}
has the same distribution with
y\cdot z_{2}|y
. Therefore, we complete the proof of Lemma
D.1
.
∎

Lemma D.2
.

For
y
and
\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{x}_{D}
defined in Definition
3.1
, it holds that
y
is independent with
y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j}
for all
j\in[D]
. Moreover,
y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j}\sim N(\mathbf{0},\sigma_{x}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d})
for all
j\neq j^{*}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.2
.

For simplicity of expression, we assume
\sigma_{x}=1
here, and the proof for
\sigma_{x}\neq 1
is the same.
We consider the following two cases:
j\neq j^{*}
and
j=j^{*}
.

When
j\neq j^{*}
,
y
is independent with
\mathbf{x}_{j}
. Then for any
k\in[d]
, we have
y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j}[k]\sim N(0,1)
and
y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j}[k]
is independent with
y
by Lemma
D.1
. Therefore,
y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j}
is independent with
y
since each coordinate of
y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j}
is independent with
y
. Moreover, we have
\mathbb{E}[y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j}[k]\cdot y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j}[k^{\prime}]]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{j}[k]\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j}[k^{\prime}]]=0
for any
k\neq k^{\prime}
, which finally proves that
y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j}\sim N(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{d})
.

When
j=j^{*}
, let
\mathbf{A}
be an orthogonal matrix with
\mathbf{v}^{*}
being its first column. We denote
\bm{\xi}_{2},\cdots,\bm{\xi}_{d}
the rest columns in
\mathbf{A}
, i.e.,
\mathbf{A}=[\mathbf{v}^{*},\bm{\xi}_{2},\cdots,\bm{\xi}_{d}]
. Then we have

\displaystyle y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}

\displaystyle=y\cdot\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}=y\cdot[\mathbf{v}^{*},\bm{\xi}_{2},\cdots,\bm{\xi}_{d}]\cdot[\langle\mathbf{v}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}\rangle,\langle\bm{\xi}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}\rangle,\cdots,\langle\bm{\xi}_{d},\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}\rangle]^{\top}

\displaystyle=y\cdot\langle\mathbf{v}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}\rangle\cdot\mathbf{v}^{*}+\sum_{i=2}^{d}y\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{i},\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}\rangle\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i}.

By orthogonality among
\mathbf{v}^{*},\bm{\xi}_{2},\cdots,\bm{\xi}_{d}
, we obtain that
\langle\mathbf{v}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}\rangle
and
\langle\bm{\xi}_{i},\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}\rangle
for
i\in\{2,\cdots,d\}
are all independent random variables with standard normal distribution, which further indicates that
y
is independent with
\langle\bm{\xi}_{i},\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}\rangle
for all
i\in\{2,\cdots,d\}
. Then by Lemma
D.1
, we can obtain that
y
is independent with
\sum_{i=2}^{d}y\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{i},\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}\rangle\cdot\mathbf{v}_{i}
. Besides, note that
y
is the sign of the standard normal random variable
\langle\mathbf{v}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}\rangle
. From Lemma
D.1
, we have that
y
is also independent with
y\cdot\langle\mathbf{v}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}\rangle
. Hence
y
is independent with
y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j^{*}}
. This completes the proof of Lemma
D.2
.
∎

Lemma D.3
.

For
y
and
\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{x}_{D}
defined in Definition
3.1
, it holds that
y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{1},y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{2},\cdots,y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{D}
are mutually independent.

Proof of Lemma
D.3
.

For any
j_{1},j_{2}\in[D]
with
j_{1}\neq j_{2}
, then at least one of
j_{1}
and
j_{2}
is not
j^{*}
. W.L.O.G., we assume
j_{2}\neq j^{*}
. Then by Definition
3.1
,
\mathbf{x}_{j_{2}}
is independent both with
y
and
\mathbf{x}_{j_{1}}
, hence it’s independent with the product
y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j_{1}}
. On the other hand, by applying Lemma
D.2
,
y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j_{1}}
is independent with
y
. Therefore, we finally prove that
y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j_{1}}
is independent with
y\cdot\mathbf{x}_{j_{2}}
.
∎

D.2
Calculation details of expectations

Lemma D.4
.

Let
\mathbf{x}\sim N(0,\sigma_{x}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d})
, for any fixed
\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, it holds that
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{x}\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}\big(\langle\mathbf{x},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\big]=\sigma_{x}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}}{\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\|_{2}}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.4
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}=[\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}/\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\|_{2},\bm{\xi}^{\prime}_{2},\cdots,\bm{\xi}^{\prime}_{d}]
be an orthogonal matrix, which is defined similarly with Lemma
D.1
. Then by a similar method, we obtain that,

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}\big(\langle\mathbf{x},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)=\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}\big(\langle\mathbf{x},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\cdot\Big\langle\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}}{\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\|_{2}},\mathbf{x}\Big\rangle\cdot\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}}{\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\|_{2}}+\sum_{i=2}^{d}\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}\big(\langle\mathbf{x},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}^{\prime}_{i},\mathbf{x}\rangle\cdot\bm{\xi}^{\prime}_{i}.

Note that
\langle\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}}{\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\|_{2}},\mathbf{x}\rangle
and
\langle\bm{\xi}_{i},\mathbf{x}\rangle
for
i\in\{2,\cdots,d\}
are i.i.d normal random variables with mean 0 and variance
\sigma_{x}^{2}
, therefore we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{x}\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}\big(\langle\mathbf{x},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\Big]=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}\big(\langle\mathbf{x},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\rangle\big)\cdot\Big\langle\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}}{\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\|_{2}},\mathbf{x}\Big\rangle\bigg]\cdot\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}}{\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\|_{2}}=\sigma_{x}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}}{\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\|_{2}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.5
.

Let
z_{1}\sim N(0,\sigma_{1}^{2})
and a fixed scalar
a
, then it holds that

1.

If
a<0
, then

\displaystyle\max\bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi e}}e^{\sigma_{1}a},\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\Big(\frac{1}{-\sigma_{1}a}-\frac{1}{-\sigma_{1}^{3}a^{3}}\Big)\bigg\}\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(a|z_{1}|)\big]\leq\min\bigg\{2,\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{1}{-\sigma_{1}a}\bigg\}.

2.

If
a\geq 0
, then

\displaystyle e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(a|z_{1}|)\big]\leq 2e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}.

Proof of Lemma
D.5
.

The probability density function for
|z_{1}|
is
f_{|z_{1}|}(x)=\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}e^{-x^{2}/(2\sigma_{1}^{2})}\mathbf{1}_{\{x\geq 0\}}
. By this density function, we can calculate
\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(a|z_{1}|)\big]
as,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(a|z_{1}|)\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma_{1}^{2}}+ax}\text{d}x=\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\big(\frac{x}{\sigma_{1}}-\sigma_{1}a\big)^{2}}\text{d}x=2e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\mathbb{P}(z\geq-\sigma_{1}a),

where
z
is a standard Gaussian random variable. If
a\geq 0
, it holds that
\frac{1}{2}\leq\mathbb{P}(z\geq-\sigma_{1}a)\leq 1
. Then we can obtain that

\displaystyle e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(a|z_{1}|)\big]\leq 2e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}.

When
a<0
, by applying the tail-bound of standard Gaussian random variables and Mills ratio simultaneously, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{-\sigma_{1}a}-\frac{1}{-\sigma_{1}^{3}a^{3}}\Big)\leq\mathbb{P}(z\geq-\sigma_{1}a)\leq e^{-\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\min\bigg\{1,\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{1}{-\sigma_{1}a}\bigg\}.

Therefore, we derive that,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\Big(\frac{1}{-\sigma_{1}a}-\frac{1}{-\sigma_{1}^{3}a^{3}}\Big)\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(a|z_{1}|)\big]\leq\min\bigg\{2,\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{1}{-\sigma_{1}a}\bigg\}.

Besides, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(z\geq-\sigma_{1}a)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{-\sigma_{1}a}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2}}\text{d}x\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{(-\sigma_{1}a+1)^{2}}{2}},

which further implies that
\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(a|z_{1}|)\big]\geq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi e}}e^{\sigma_{1}a}
. Combining all preceding results, we finish the proof.
∎

Lemma D.6
.

Let
z_{1}\sim N(0,\sigma_{1}^{2})
and two fixed positive scalars
a,b
, then it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(-a|z_{1}|)\mathbf{1}_{|z_{1}|\leq b}\big]\geq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{3}a^{3}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a+b/\sigma_{1}}\exp\Big(-ab-\frac{b^{2}}{2\sigma_{1}^{2}}\Big)\bigg).

Proof of Lemma
D.6
.

The probability density function for
|z_{1}|
is
f_{|z_{1}|}(x)=\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}e^{-x^{2}/(2\sigma_{1}^{2})}\mathbf{1}_{\{x\geq 0\}}
. By this density function, we can calculate
\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(-a|z_{1}|)\mathbf{1}_{|z_{1}|\leq b}\big]
as,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(-a|z_{1}|)\mathbf{1}_{|z_{1}|\leq b}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\int_{0}^{b}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma_{1}^{2}}-ax}\text{d}x=\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\int_{0}^{b}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\big(\frac{x}{\sigma_{1}}+\sigma_{1}a\big)^{2}}\text{d}x

\displaystyle=2e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\Big(\mathbb{P}(z\geq\sigma_{1}a)-\mathbb{P}(z\geq\sigma_{1}a+b/\sigma_{1})\Big),

where
z
is a standard Gaussian random variable. By applying the Mills ratio, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(z\geq-\sigma_{1}a)\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{3}a^{3}}\Big),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(z\geq\sigma_{1}a+b/\sigma_{1})\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}(\sigma_{1}a+b/\sigma_{1})}e^{-\frac{(\sigma_{1}a+b/\sigma_{1})^{2}}{2}}

Therefore, we finally derive that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(-a|z_{1}|)\mathbf{1}_{|z_{1}|\leq b}\big]\geq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{3}a^{3}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a+b/\sigma_{1}}\exp\Big(-ab-\frac{b^{2}}{2\sigma_{1}^{2}}\Big)\bigg),

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.7
.

Let
z_{1}\sim N(0,\sigma_{1}^{2})
and a fixed scalar
a
, then it holds that

1.

If
a\geq 0
, then

\displaystyle lb_{1}(\sigma_{1},a)\leq\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}|\exp(-a|z_{1}|)\big]\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a^{2}},

(D.1)

where
lb_{1}(\sigma_{1},a)=\max\bigg\{\sigma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}-2\sigma_{1}^{2}a,\frac{\sigma_{1}}{2\sqrt{2e\pi}}e^{-\sigma_{1}a},\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a^{2}}-\frac{3}{\sigma_{1}^{3}a^{4}}\Big)\bigg\}
.

2.

If
a<0
, then

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}-\sigma_{1}^{2}ae^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\leq\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}|\exp(-a|z_{1}|)\big]\leq\sigma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}-2\sigma_{1}^{2}ae^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}.

(D.2)

Proof of Lemma
D.7
.

Similar to Lemma
D.5
, we can calculate
\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}|\exp(-a|z_{1}|)\big]
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}|\exp(-a|z_{1}|)\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}xe^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma_{1}^{2}}-ax}\text{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}xe^{-\frac{1}{2}\big(\frac{x}{\sigma_{1}}+\sigma_{1}a\big)^{2}}\text{d}x

\displaystyle=\sigma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}xe^{-\frac{1}{2}(x+\sigma_{1}a)^{2}}\text{d}x

\displaystyle=\sigma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\Bigg[-\sigma_{1}a\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(x+\sigma_{1}a)^{2}}\text{d}x+\int_{0}^{\infty}(x+\sigma_{1}a)e^{-\frac{1}{2}(x+\sigma_{1}a)^{2}}\text{d}x\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\sigma_{1}\Bigg[\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}-2\sigma_{1}ae^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\mathbb{P}(z\geq\sigma_{1}a)\Bigg],

(D.3)

where
z
is a standard Gaussian random variable. When
a\geq 0
, by applying tail-bound of standard Gaussian random variables and Mills ratio simultaneously, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{3}a^{3}}\Big)\leq\mathbb{P}(z\geq\sigma_{1}a)\leq e^{-\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\min\bigg\{1,\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{3}a^{3}}+\frac{3}{\sigma_{1}^{5}a^{5}}\Big)\bigg\}.

(D.4)

Applying the result of (
D.4
) into (
D.3
), we finally get

\displaystyle\max\Bigg\{\sigma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}-2\sigma_{1}^{2}a,\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a^{2}}-\frac{3}{\sigma_{1}^{3}a^{4}}\bigg)\Bigg\}\leq\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}|\exp(-a|z_{1}|)\big]\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a^{2}}.

(D.5)

Besides, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}|\exp(-a|z_{1}|)\big]

\displaystyle=\sigma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}xe^{-\frac{1}{2}(x+\sigma_{1}a)^{2}}\text{d}x

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\int_{1/2}^{1}xe^{-\frac{1}{2}(x+\sigma_{1}a)^{2}}\text{d}x\geq\frac{\sigma_{1}}{2\sqrt{2e\pi}}e^{-\sigma_{1}a}.

(D.6)

Combining the results of (
D.5
) and (
D.6
), we finishes the proof of (
D.1
). When
a<0
, it’s obvious that
1/2\leq\mathbb{P}(z\geq\sigma_{1}a)\leq 1
. Replacing this results in (
D.3
), we finish the proof for (
D.2
)
∎

Lemma D.8
.

Let
z_{1}\sim N(0,\sigma_{1}^{2})
and a non-negative fixed scalar
a
, then it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[z_{1}^{2}\exp(a|z_{1}|)\big]\leq 2\sigma_{1}^{2}(a^{2}\sigma_{1}^{2}+1)e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}

Proof of Lemma
D.8
.

Based on the density function of
|z_{1}|
, we can calculate that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[z_{1}^{2}\exp(a|z_{1}|)\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}x^{2}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma_{1}^{2}}+ax}\text{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}x^{2}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\big(\frac{x}{\sigma_{1}}-\sigma_{1}a\big)^{2}}\text{d}x

\displaystyle=\sigma_{1}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\int_{-a\sigma_{1}}^{\infty}(x+a\sigma_{1})^{2}e^{-\frac{1}{2}x^{2}}\text{d}x

\displaystyle\leq 2\sigma_{1}^{2}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[(z+a\sigma_{1})^{2}\big]=2\sigma_{1}^{2}(a^{2}\sigma_{1}^{2}+1)e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}}{2}},

where
z
is a standard Gaussian random variable. This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.9
.

For any Gaussian random variable
z
with mean 0, it holds that
\mathbb{E}[-\ell^{\prime}(z)]\geq 1/4
.

Proof of Lemma
D.9
.

It can be derived that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[-\ell^{\prime}(z)]=\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(z)\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq 0\}}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(z)\mathbf{1}_{\{z>0\}}\big]\geq\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(z)\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq 0\}}\big]\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq 0\}}\big]=\frac{1}{4},

which finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.10
.

Let
z_{1}\sim N(0,\sigma_{1}^{2})
and
z_{2}\sim N(0,\sigma_{2}^{2})
be two independent Gaussian random variables, and
a,b
be two scalars. Then it holds that,

1.

If
a\geq 0
, then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}lb_{1}(\sigma_{1},a)\leq-\mathbb{E}\big[\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2})|z_{1}|\big]\leq ub_{1}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},a,b),

(D.7)

where
lb_{1}(\sigma_{1},a)=\max\bigg\{\sigma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}-2\sigma_{1}^{2}a,\frac{\sigma_{1}}{2\sqrt{2e\pi}}e^{-\sigma_{1}a},\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a^{2}}-\frac{3}{\sigma_{1}^{3}a^{4}}\Big)\bigg\}
as defined in Lemma
D.7
, and
ub_{1}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},a,b)=\min\Big\{\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a^{2}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{2}^{2}b^{2}}{2}},\sigma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\Big\}
.

2.

If
a<0
, then

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\leq-\mathbb{E}\big[\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2})|z_{1}|\big]\leq\sigma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}.

(D.8)

Proof of Lemma
D.10
.

By the law of total expectation, we have
-\mathbb{E}\big[\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2})|z_{1}|\big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2})|z_{1}|\big|z_{2}\big]\Big]
. Since
z_{1}
,
z_{2}
are independent, we still have
z_{1}|z_{2}\sim N(0,\sigma_{1})
. We first prove the
case for
a\geq 0
. The lower-bound can be calculated as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2})|z_{1}|\big|z_{2}\big]\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{|z_{1}|}{1+\exp{(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2})}}\bigg|z_{2}\bigg]\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{|z_{1}|}{\exp{(a|z_{1}|)}+\exp{(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2})}}\bigg|z_{2}\bigg]\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}[-\ell^{\prime}(bz_{2})]\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}|\exp(-a|z_{1}|)\big]\geq\frac{1}{4}lb_{1}(\sigma_{1},a),

(D.9)

where the last inequality holds by applying Lemma
D.7
and Lemma
D.9
, and
lb_{1}(\sigma_{1},a)
is defined in Lemma
D.7
.
On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\big[\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2})|z_{1}|\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{|z_{1}|}{1+\exp{(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2})}}\bigg]\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{|z_{1}|}{\exp{(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2})}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}|\exp(-a|z_{1}|)\big]\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(-bz_{2})\big]\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a^{2}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{2}^{2}b^{2}}{2}},

(D.10)

where the last inequality holds by applying Lemma
D.7
and moment-generating function of Gaussian random variables. Besides, we also have

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\big[\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2})|z_{1}|\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}|\big]=\sigma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}.

(D.11)

Combining (
D.9
), (
D.10
) and (
D.11
), we finish the proof for (
D.7
). Next, we prove the case for
a<0
, and (
D.11
) still holds for
a<0
. Besides by Lemma
D.9
, we can obatin that

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\big[\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2})|z_{1}|\big]\geq-\mathbb{E}\big[\ell^{\prime}(bz_{2})\big]\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}|\big]\geq\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}.

(D.12)

Combining (
D.11
) and (
D.12
), we finish the proof for (
D.8
).
∎

Lemma D.11
.

Let
z_{1}\sim N(0,\sigma_{1}^{2})
,
z_{2}\sim N(0,\sigma_{2}^{2})
and
z_{3}\sim N(0,\sigma_{3}^{2})
be three independent Gaussian random variables, and
a,b,c
be three non-negative scalars. Then it holds that

\displaystyle lb_{2}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},a,b,c)\leq-\mathbb{E}[\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})z_{3}]\leq ub_{2}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},a,b,c),

(D.13)

where

\displaystyle lb_{2}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},a,b,c)=\max\Bigg\{-\frac{c\sigma_{3}^{2}}{4},-\frac{\sigma_{3}}{\sqrt{2\pi}},-2\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{\sigma_{3}^{2}c}{\sigma_{2}b}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}+\sigma_{3}^{2}c^{2}}{2}},-\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{\sigma_{3}^{2}c}{\sigma_{1}a}e^{\frac{\sigma_{2}^{2}b^{2}}{2}+\frac{\sigma_{3}^{2}c^{2}}{2}}\Bigg\},

and

\displaystyle ub_{2}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},a,b,c)=-\frac{\sigma_{3}}{3\pi\sigma_{2}b}\max\bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi e}}e^{-\sigma_{1}a},\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{3}a^{3}}\Big)\bigg\}e^{-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{2}^{2}b^{2}}-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{3}^{2}c^{2}}}.

Proof of Lemma
D.11
.

Similarly, by the law of total expectation, we have
-\mathbb{E}\big[\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})z_{3}\big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})z_{3}\big|z_{1},z_{2}\big]\Big]
. Notice that
-\ell^{\prime}
is a decreasing function and
z_{3}
has a zero-centered symmetric density function. Hence, we obtain that
\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})z_{3}\big|z_{1},z_{2}\big]\leq 0
. Specifically, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})z_{3}\big|z_{1},z_{2}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})z_{3}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}\geq 0\}}\big|z_{1},z_{2}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})z_{3}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}<0\}}\big|z_{1},z_{2}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3})+\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})\big)z_{3}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}\geq 0\}}\big|z_{1},z_{2}\Big].

(D.14)

Since
-\ell^{\prime}
is Lipschitz continuous with
\frac{1}{4}
and the value of
-\ell^{\prime}
is always in
(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3})+\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})\big)z_{3}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}\geq 0\}}\big|z_{1},z_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\min\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big[cz_{3}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}\geq 0\}}/2\big|z_{1},z_{2}\big],\mathbb{E}\big[z_{3}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}\geq 0\}}\big|z_{1},z_{2}\big]\Big\}=\max\Big\{-\frac{c\sigma_{3}^{2}}{4},-\frac{\sigma_{3}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big\}.

(D.15)

On the other hand, we also have

\displaystyle-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3})+\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3}}}-\frac{1}{1+e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3}}}=\frac{e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}}(e^{cz_{3}}-e^{-cz_{3}})}{\big(1+e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3}}\big)\big(1+e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3}}\big)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}}(e^{cz_{3}}-e^{-cz_{3}})}{\big(e^{a|z_{1}|}+e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3}}\big)\big(e^{a|z_{1}|}+e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3}}\big)}=e^{-a|z_{1}|}\frac{e^{cz_{3}}-e^{-cz_{3}}}{e^{cz_{3}}+e^{-cz_{3}}+e^{bz_{2}}+e^{-bz_{2}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle e^{-a|z_{1}|}\frac{e^{cz_{3}}-e^{-cz_{3}}}{e^{cz_{3}}+e^{-cz_{3}}+e^{bz_{2}}+e^{-bz_{2}}}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}\geq 1/c\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z_{2}|\leq 1/b\}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle e^{-a|z_{1}|}\frac{e-e^{-1}}{2(e+e^{-1})}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}\geq 1/c\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z_{2}|\leq 1/b\}}\geq\frac{1}{3}e^{-a|z_{1}|}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}\geq 1/c\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z_{2}|\leq 1/b\}}.

(D.16)

By applying result of (
D.16
) in (
D.14
) and Lemma
D.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})z_{3}\big]\leq-\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-a|z_{1}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{3}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}\geq 1/c\}}\big]\mathbb{P}(|z_{2}|\leq 1/b)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma_{3}}{3\pi\sigma_{2}b}\max\bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi e}}e^{-\sigma_{1}a},\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{3}a^{3}}\Big)\bigg\}e^{-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{2}^{2}b^{2}}-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{3}^{2}c^{2}}},

(D.17)

where the last inequality holds because
\mathbb{E}\big[z_{3}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}\geq 1/c\}}\big]=\frac{1}{\sigma_{3}\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{1/c}^{\infty}xe^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma_{3}^{2}}}\text{d}x=\frac{\sigma_{3}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{1}{2c^{2}\sigma_{3}^{2}}}
and
\mathbb{P}(|z_{2}|\leq 1/b)=\frac{1}{\sigma_{2}\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{-1/b}^{1/b}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma_{2}^{2}}}\text{d}x\geq\frac{2}{b\sigma_{2}\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{2}^{2}b^{2}}}
. Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3})+\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3}}}-\frac{1}{1+e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3}}}=\frac{e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}}(e^{cz_{3}}-e^{-cz_{3}})}{\big(1+e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3}}\big)\big(1+e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3}}\big)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}}(e^{cz_{3}}-e^{-cz_{3}})}{\big(1+e^{bz_{2}-cz_{3}}\big)\big(1+e^{bz_{2}+cz_{3}}\big)}=e^{a|z_{1}|}\frac{e^{cz_{3}}-e^{-cz_{3}}}{e^{cz_{3}}+e^{-cz_{3}}+e^{bz_{2}}+e^{-bz_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{a|z_{1}|}\frac{e^{cz_{3}}-e^{-cz_{3}}}{e^{bz_{2}}+e^{-bz_{2}}}\leq e^{a|z_{1}|}e^{-b|z_{2}|}\big(e^{cz_{3}}-e^{-cz_{3}}\big).

(D.18)

By applying result of (
D.18
), Lemma
D.5
and Lemma
D.7
in (
D.14
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})z_{3}\big]

\displaystyle\geq-\mathbb{E}\big[e^{a|z_{1}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-b|z_{2}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{3}(e^{cz_{3}}-e^{-cz_{3}})\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}\geq 0\}}\big]

\displaystyle\geq-2\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{\sigma_{3}^{2}c}{\sigma_{2}b}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a^{2}+\sigma_{3}^{2}c^{2}}{2}}.

(D.19)

Moreover, we can also obtain that

\displaystyle-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3})+\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3}}}-\frac{1}{1+e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3}}}=\frac{e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}}(e^{cz_{3}}-e^{-cz_{3}})}{\big(1+e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3}}\big)\big(1+e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3}}\big)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}}(e^{cz_{3}}-e^{-cz_{3}})}{e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}-cz_{3}}e^{a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3}}}=e^{-a|z_{1}|}e^{-bz_{2}}\big(e^{cz_{3}}-e^{-cz_{3}}\big).

(D.20)

By applying result of (
D.20
), Lemma
D.5
and Lemma
D.7
in (
D.14
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3})z_{3}\big]

\displaystyle\geq-\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-a|z_{1}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-bz_{2}}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{3}(e^{cz_{3}}-e^{-cz_{3}})\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}\geq 0\}}\big]

\displaystyle\geq-\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{\sigma_{3}^{2}c}{\sigma_{1}a}e^{\frac{\sigma_{2}^{2}b^{2}}{2}+\frac{\sigma_{3}^{2}c^{2}}{2}}.

(D.21)

Combining (
D.15
), (
D.17
), (
D.19
), and (
D.21
), we finish the proof for (
D.13
).
∎

The proofs for the following Lemmas are very similar to that of Lemma
D.11
, while we still include them here for completeness.

Lemma D.12
.

Let
z_{1}\sim N(0,\sigma_{1}^{2})
,
z_{2}\sim N(0,\sigma_{2}^{2})
,
z_{3}\sim N(0,\sigma_{3}^{2})
, and
z_{4}\sim N(0,\sigma_{4}^{2})
be four independent Gaussian random variables, and
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4}
be four non-negative scalars. Then it holds that

\displaystyle lb_{3}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4},a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4})

\displaystyle\leq-\mathbb{E}[\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4})z_{3}^{2}z_{4}]

\displaystyle\leq ub_{3}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4},a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4}),

(D.22)

where

\displaystyle lb_{3}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4},a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4})=\max\Bigg\{-\frac{a_{4}\sigma_{3}^{2}\sigma_{4}^{2}}{4},-\frac{\sigma_{3}^{2}\sigma_{4}}{\sqrt{2\pi}},-4\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{\sigma_{3}^{2}\sigma_{4}^{2}a_{4}(a_{3}^{2}\sigma_{3}^{2}+1)}{\sigma_{2}a_{2}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a_{1}^{2}+\sigma_{3}^{2}a_{3}^{2}+\sigma_{4}^{2}a_{4}^{2}}{2}}\Bigg\},

and

\displaystyle ub_{3}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4},a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4})=-\frac{\sigma_{4}}{192\pi^{3}a_{2}a_{3}^{3}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}\max\bigg\{e^{-\sigma_{1}a_{1}-1/2},\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a_{1}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{3}a_{1}^{3}}\bigg\}e^{-\frac{1}{8\sigma_{2}^{2}a_{2}^{2}}-\frac{1}{8\sigma_{3}^{2}a_{3}^{2}}-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{4}^{2}a_{4}^{2}}}.

Proof of Lemma
D.12
.

By the law of total expectation, we have
-\mathbb{E}[\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3}+dz_{4})z_{3}^{2}z_{4}]=\mathbb{E}\Big[z_{3}^{2}\mathbb{E}[-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3}+dz_{4})z_{4}\big|z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}]\Big]
. Notice that
-\ell^{\prime}
is a decreasing function and
z_{4}
has a zero-centered symmetric density function. Hence, we obtain that
\mathbb{E}[-\ell^{\prime}(a|z_{1}|+bz_{2}+cz_{3}+dz_{4})z_{4}\big|z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}]\leq 0
. Specifically, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[-\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4})z_{4}\big|z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4})z_{4}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{4}\geq 0\}}\big|z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\big]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4})z_{4}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{4}<0\}}\big|z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(-\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}-a_{4}z_{4})+\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4})\big)z_{4}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{4}\geq 0\}}\big|z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\Big].

(D.23)

Since
-\ell^{\prime}
is Lipschitz continuous with
\frac{1}{4}
and the value of
-\ell^{\prime}
is always in
(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(-\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}-a_{4}z_{4})+\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4})\big)z_{4}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{4}\geq 0\}}\big|z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\Big]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\min\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big[a_{4}z_{4}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{4}\geq 0\}}/2\big|z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\big],\mathbb{E}\big[z_{4}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{4}\geq 0\}}\big|z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\big]\Big\}=\max\Big\{-\frac{a_{4}\sigma_{4}^{2}}{4},-\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big\}.

Then we have

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}[\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4})z_{3}^{2}z_{4}]\geq\max\Big\{-\frac{a_{4}\sigma_{4}^{2}}{4},-\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big\}\mathbb{E}[z_{3}^{2}]=\max\Big\{-\frac{a_{4}\sigma_{3}^{2}\sigma_{4}^{2}}{4},-\frac{\sigma_{3}^{2}\sigma_{4}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big\}.

(D.24)

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle-\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}-a_{4}z_{4})+\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}-a_{4}z_{4}}}-\frac{1}{1+e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}}(e^{a_{4}z_{4}}-e^{-a_{4}z_{4}})}{\big(1+e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}-a_{4}z_{4}}\big)\big(1+e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4}}\big)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}}(e^{a_{4}z_{4}}-e^{-a_{4}z_{4}})}{\big(e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|}+e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}-a_{4}z_{4}}\big)\big(e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|}+e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4}}\big)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-a_{1}|z_{1}|}\frac{e^{a_{4}z_{4}}-e^{-a_{4}z_{4}}}{e^{a_{4}z_{4}}+e^{-a_{4}z_{4}}+e^{a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}}+e^{-a_{2}z_{2}-a_{3}z_{3}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle e^{-a_{1}|z_{1}|}\frac{e^{a_{4}z_{4}}-e^{-a_{4}z_{4}}}{e^{a_{4}z_{4}}+e^{-a_{4}z_{4}}+e^{a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}}+e^{-a_{2}z_{2}-a_{4}z_{3}}}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{4}\geq\frac{1}{a_{4}}\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z_{2}|\leq\frac{1}{2a_{2}}\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z_{3}|\leq\frac{1}{2a_{3}}\}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle e^{-a_{1}|z_{1}|}\frac{e-e^{-1}}{2(e+e^{-1})}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{4}\geq\frac{1}{a_{4}}\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z_{2}|\leq\frac{1}{2a_{2}}\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z_{3}|\leq\frac{1}{2a_{3}}\}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}e^{-a_{1}|z_{1}|}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{4}\geq\frac{1}{a_{4}}\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z_{2}|\leq\frac{1}{2a_{2}}\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z_{3}|\leq\frac{1}{2a_{3}}\}}.

(D.25)

By applying result of (
D.25
) in (
D.23
) and Lemma
D.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[-\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4})z_{4}\big]\leq-\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-a_{1}|z_{1}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{4}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{4}\geq\frac{1}{a_{4}}\}}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{3}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z_{3}|\leq\frac{1}{2a_{3}}\}}\big]\mathbb{P}\Big(|z_{2}|\leq\frac{1}{2a_{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma_{4}}{192\pi^{3}a_{2}a_{3}^{3}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}\max\bigg\{e^{-\sigma_{1}a_{1}-1/2},\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}a_{1}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{3}a_{1}^{3}}\bigg\}e^{-\frac{1}{8\sigma_{2}^{2}a_{2}^{2}}-\frac{1}{8\sigma_{3}^{2}a_{3}^{2}}-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{4}^{2}a_{4}^{2}}},

(D.26)

where the last inequality holds because
\mathbb{E}\big[z_{4}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{4}\geq 1/a_{4}\}}\big]=\frac{1}{\sigma_{4}\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{1/a_{4}}^{\infty}xe^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma_{4}^{2}}}\text{d}x=\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{1}{2a_{4}^{2}\sigma_{4}^{2}}}
,
\mathbb{E}\big[z_{3}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z_{3}|\leq\frac{1}{2a_{3}}\}}\big]=\frac{1}{\sigma_{3}\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{-\frac{1}{2a_{3}}}^{\frac{1}{2a_{3}}}x^{2}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma_{3}^{2}}}\text{d}x\geq\frac{1}{32a_{3}^{3}\sigma_{3}\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{1}{8\sigma_{3}^{2}a_{3}^{2}}}
, and
\mathbb{P}(|z_{2}|\leq\frac{1}{2a_{2}})=\frac{1}{\sigma_{2}\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{-\frac{1}{2a_{2}}}^{\frac{1}{2a_{2}}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma_{2}^{2}}}\text{d}x\leq\frac{1}{a_{2}\sigma_{2}\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{1}{8\sigma_{2}^{2}a_{2}^{2}}}
. Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle-\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}-a_{4}z_{4})+\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}-a_{4}z_{4}}}-\frac{1}{1+e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}}(e^{a_{4}z_{4}}-e^{-a_{4}z_{4}})}{\big(1+e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}-a_{4}z_{4}}\big)\big(1+e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4}}\big)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}}(e^{a_{4}z_{4}}-e^{-a_{4}z_{4}})}{\big(1+e^{a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}-a_{4}z_{4}}\big)\big(1+e^{a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4}}\big)}=e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|}\frac{e^{a_{4}z_{4}}-e^{-a_{4}z_{4}}}{e^{a_{4}z_{4}}+e^{-a_{4}z_{4}}+e^{a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}}+e^{-a_{2}z_{2}-a_{3}z_{3}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|}\frac{e^{a_{4}z_{4}}-e^{-a_{4}z_{4}}}{e^{a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}}+e^{-a_{2}z_{2}-a_{3}z_{3}}}\leq e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|}e^{-|a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}|}(e^{a_{4}z_{4}}-e^{-a_{4}z_{4}})\leq e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|}e^{-a_{2}|z_{2}|}e^{a_{3}|z_{3}|}(e^{a_{4}z_{4}}-e^{-a_{4}z_{4}})

(D.27)

By applying result of (
D.27
), Lemma
D.5
, Lemma
D.7
and Lemma
D.8
in (
D.23
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[-\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4})z_{3}^{2}z_{4}]

\displaystyle\geq-\mathbb{E}\big[e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-a_{2}|z_{2}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{3}^{2}e^{a_{3}|z_{3}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{4}(e^{a_{4}z_{4}}-e^{-a_{4}z_{4}})\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{4}\geq 0\}}\big]

\displaystyle\geq-4\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{\sigma_{3}^{2}\sigma_{4}^{2}a_{4}(a_{3}^{2}\sigma_{3}^{2}+1)}{\sigma_{2}a_{2}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a_{1}^{2}+\sigma_{3}^{2}a_{3}^{2}+\sigma_{4}^{2}a_{4}^{2}}{2}}.

(D.28)

Combining (
D.24
), (
D.26
), and (
D.28
), we finish the proof for (
D.22
).
∎

Lemma D.13
.

Let
z_{1}\sim N(0,\sigma_{1}^{2})
,
z_{2}\sim N(0,\sigma_{2}^{2})
,
z_{3}\sim N(0,\sigma_{3}^{2}),z_{4}\sim N(0,\sigma_{4}^{2})
and
z_{5}\sim N(0,\sigma_{5}^{2})
be five independent Gaussian random variables, and
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},a_{5}
be five non-negative scalars. Then it holds that

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}[\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4}+a_{5}z_{5})z_{3}z_{4}z_{5}]\geq-8\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{2}a_{2}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a_{1}^{2}}{2}}\prod_{i=3}^{5}\bigg(\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}+\sigma_{i}^{2}a_{i}e^{\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}a_{i}^{2}}{2}}\bigg);

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}[\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4}+a_{5}z_{5})z_{3}z_{4}z_{5}]\leq 8\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{2}a_{2}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a_{1}^{2}}{2}}\prod_{i=3}^{5}\bigg(\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}+\sigma_{i}^{2}a_{i}e^{\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}a_{i}^{2}}{2}}\bigg).

(D.29)

Proof of Lemma
D.13
.

By the law of total expectation, we have

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}[\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4}+a_{5}z_{5})z_{3}z_{4}z_{5}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}[-\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4}+a_{5}z_{5})z_{3}z_{4}z_{5}\big|z_{3},z_{4},z_{5}]\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}[-\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4}+a_{5}z_{5})z_{3}z_{4}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}z_{4}\geq 0\}}z_{5}\big|z_{3},z_{4},z_{5}]\Big]}_{\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\textcircled{\fontsize{9pt}{0}\fontfamily{phv}\selectfont 1}}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}[-\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4}+a_{5}z_{5})z_{3}z_{4}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}z_{4}<0\}}z_{5}\big|z_{3},z_{4},z_{5}]\Big]}_{\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\textcircled{\fontsize{9pt}{0}\fontfamily{phv}\selectfont 2}}}

Since
-\ell^{\prime}
is a decreasing function and
z_{5}
has a zero-centered symmetric density function. We can conclude that
\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\textcircled{\fontsize{9pt}{0}\fontfamily{phv}\selectfont 1}}\leq 0
and
\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\textcircled{\fontsize{9pt}{0}\fontfamily{phv}\selectfont 2}}\geq 0
, and

\displaystyle\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\textcircled{\fontsize{9pt}{0}\fontfamily{phv}\selectfont 1}}\leq-\mathbb{E}[\ell^{\prime}(a_{1}|z_{1}|+a_{2}z_{2}+a_{3}z_{3}+a_{4}z_{4}+a_{5}z_{5})z_{3}z_{4}z_{5}]\leq\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\textcircled{\fontsize{9pt}{0}\fontfamily{phv}\selectfont 2}}.

Next, we provide a lower bound for
\fontsize{9pt}{0}\fontfamily{phv}\selectfont1⃝
and an upper bound for
\fontsize{9pt}{0}\fontfamily{phv}\selectfont2⃝
. Similar to the proof of Lemma
D.12
, we have

\fontsize{9pt}{0}\fontfamily{phv}\selectfont1⃝

\displaystyle\geq-\mathbb{E}\big[e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-a_{2}|z_{2}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{3}z_{4}e^{a_{3}|z_{3}|+a_{4}|z_{4}|}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}z_{4}\geq 0\}}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{5}e^{a_{5}z_{5}}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{5}\geq 0\}}\big]

\displaystyle=-2\mathbb{E}\big[e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-a_{2}|z_{2}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{3}e^{a_{3}z_{3}}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}\geq 0\}}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{4}e^{a_{4}z_{4}}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{4}\geq 0\}}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{5}e^{a_{5}z_{5}}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{5}\geq 0\}}\big]

\displaystyle\geq-8\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{2}a_{2}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a_{1}^{2}}{2}}\prod_{i=3}^{5}\bigg(\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}+\sigma_{i}^{2}a_{i}e^{\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}a_{i}^{2}}{2}}\bigg).

The last inequality is derived by applying Lemma
D.5
, Lemma
D.7
and Lemma
D.8
. On the other hand, we can obtain that

\fontsize{9pt}{0}\fontfamily{phv}\selectfont2⃝

\displaystyle\leq-\mathbb{E}\big[e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-a_{2}|z_{2}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{3}z_{4}e^{a_{3}|z_{3}|+a_{4}|z_{4}|}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}z_{4}<0\}}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{5}e^{a_{5}z_{5}}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{5}\geq 0\}}\big]

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}\big[e^{a_{1}|z_{1}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-a_{2}|z_{2}|}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{3}e^{a_{3}z_{3}}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{3}\geq 0\}}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{4}e^{a_{4}z_{4}}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{4}\geq 0\}}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[z_{5}e^{a_{5}z_{5}}\mathbf{1}_{\{z_{5}\geq 0\}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq 8\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{2}a_{2}}e^{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}a_{1}^{2}}{2}}\prod_{i=3}^{5}\bigg(\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}+\sigma_{i}^{2}a_{i}e^{\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}a_{i}^{2}}{2}}\bigg).

This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.14
.

Let
z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}
be three standard Gaussian random variables, and
z_{i}
,
z_{j}
are either independent or
z_{i}=z_{j}
for
i\neq j
. Then it holds that
\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}z_{2}z_{3}|\big]\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.13
.

There are three possible cases:

1.

z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}
are all independent. Then
\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}z_{2}z_{3}|\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}|\big]\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{2}|\big]\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{3}|\big]=\frac{2}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}

2.

Two of
z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}
are equal while another one is independent with these two random variables. W.L.O.G, we assume
z_{1}=z_{2}
and
z_{3}
is independent with both
z_{1},z_{2}
. Then
\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}z_{2}z_{3}|\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[z_{1}^{2}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{3}|\big]=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}

3.

z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}
are all equal. Then
\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}z_{2}z_{3}|\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[|z_{1}|^{3}\big]=2\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}

This finishes the proof.
∎

D.3
Concentration results

Lemma D.15
.

Let
z_{1},z_{2},\cdots,z_{D}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}N(0,\sigma_{x}^{2})
. Then with probability at least
1-\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{D^{3/4}}{c_{1}}e^{-c_{1}^{2}\sqrt{D}/2}
, it holds that
|z_{i}|\leq c_{1}\sigma_{x}D^{1/4}
for all
i\in[D]
and any constant
c_{1}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.15
.

By Mills ratio, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(|z_{i}|>c_{1}\sigma_{x}D^{1/4}\Big)\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{1}{c_{1}D^{1/4}}\exp\Big(-\frac{c_{1}^{2}\sqrt{D}}{2}\Big).

By applying a union bound over all
i\in[D]
, we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma D.16
.

For
\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{x}_{D}
defined in Definition
3.1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|_{2}\leq\sigma_{x}\sqrt{d}+\sigma_{x}\sqrt{D}

with probability at least
1-De^{-\frac{D}{2}}
for all
j\in[D]
.

Proof of Lemma
D.16
.

Since
\|\cdot\|_{2}
is 1-Lipschitz continuous, then by Theorem 2.26 in
Wainwright (2019)
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|_{2}-\mathbb{E}\big[\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|_{2}\big]\geq\sigma_{x}\sqrt{D}\Big)\leq e^{-\frac{D}{2}}.

Besides, by Jensen’s inequality, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|_{2}\big]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}\big]}=\sigma_{x}\sqrt{d}.

Applying a union-bound over all
j\in[D]
completes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.17
.

For
\mathbf{x}_{j}^{(i)}
defined in Definition
4.1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\big|\langle\mathbf{v}^{*},\mathbf{x}^{(i)}_{j}\rangle\big|\leq c\widetilde{\sigma}_{x}D^{1/4}

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{x}^{(i)}_{j}\big\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{x}\Big(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{D}\Big)

with probability at least
1-ne^{-c^{\prime}\sqrt{D}}
for all
i\in[n]
and
j\in[D]
, where
c
is any positive absolute constant, and
c^{\prime}
is positive constant solely depending on
c
.

Proof of Lemma
D.17
.

By definition of
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{2}}
, we have
\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}^{(i)}_{j,k}]\big|\leq\sqrt{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{x}
and
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}^{(i)}_{j,k}]^{2}\leq 2\widetilde{\sigma}_{x}^{2}
for all
i\in[n]
,
j\in[D]
and
k\in[d]
. By Hoeffding inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\bigg|\big|\langle\mathbf{v}^{*},\mathbf{x}^{(i)}_{j}\rangle\big|-\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\langle\mathbf{v}^{*},\mathbf{x}^{(i)}_{j}\rangle\big|\Big]\bigg|\geq c\widetilde{\sigma}_{x}D^{1/4}\Bigg)\leq 2\exp\Big(-\frac{c^{2}\sqrt{D}}{2}\Big).

Besides, by Bernstein’s inequality, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\bigg|\big\|\mathbf{x}^{(i)}_{j}\big\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\mathbf{x}^{(i)}_{j}\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\bigg|\geq\widetilde{2}\sigma_{x}^{2}D\Bigg)\leq 2\exp(-c^{\prime\prime}D),

(D.30)

where
c^{\prime\prime}
is an absolute constant.
Applying a union-bound over all
i\in[n]
and
j\in[D]
completes the proof.
∎

D.4
Orthogonality and norm of discrete sine transform

Lemma D.18
.

For positional encodings
\mathbf{p}_{1},\mathbf{p}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{p}_{D}
defined in (
3.1
), it holds that
\|\mathbf{p}_{j}\|=\sqrt{\frac{D+1}{2}}
for all
j\in[D]
and
\langle\mathbf{p}_{j},\mathbf{p}_{j^{\prime}}\rangle=0
for all
j\neq j^{\prime}\in[D]
.

Proof of Lemma
D.18
.

This lemma is equal to prove that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{D}\sin\bigg(\frac{\pi kj}{D+1}\bigg)\sin\bigg(\frac{\pi k^{\prime}j}{D+1}\bigg)=\frac{D+1}{2}\delta_{kk^{\prime}},\quad~\forall\ k,k^{\prime}\in[D],

where
\delta_{kk^{\prime}}=1
when
k=k^{\prime}
and
\delta_{kk^{\prime}}=0
when
k\neq k^{\prime}
. Applying the form
\sin(x)=\{\exp(ix)-\exp(-ix)\}/(2i)
, we can rewrite the left side above by

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{D}\sin\bigg(\frac{\pi kj}{D+1}\bigg)\sin\bigg(\frac{\pi k^{\prime}j}{D+1}\bigg)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}\sum_{j=0}^{D}\bigg\{\exp\bigg(\frac{i\pi(k+k^{\prime})}{D+1}j\bigg)-\exp\bigg(\frac{i\pi(k-k^{\prime})}{D+1}j\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\exp\bigg(-\frac{i\pi(k-k^{\prime})}{D+1}j\bigg)+\exp\bigg(-\frac{i\pi(k+k^{\prime})}{D+1}j\bigg)\bigg\}.

When
k+sk^{\prime}\neq 0
, the geometric partial sum shows that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{D}\exp\bigg(\pm\frac{i\pi(k+sk^{\prime})}{D+1}j\bigg)=\frac{\exp\{\pm i\pi(k+sk^{\prime})\}-1}{\exp\{\pm i\pi(k+sk^{\prime})/(D+1)\}-1},

where
s\in\{+1,-1\}
. In this case, it is required that
k+sk^{\prime}\neq 0
. We have that when
k+sk^{\prime}\neq 0
, if
k+sk^{\prime}
is even, the terms will vanish and then the equation equals
0
. When
k+sk^{\prime}
is odd, it must hold that one of
k
and
k^{\prime}
is odd and the other is even, under this case
\exp(\pm i\pi(k+sk^{\prime}))=-1
, the term compensates as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\exp\{ix\}-1}+\frac{1}{\exp\{-ix\}-1}=-1.

This indicates that the equation above equals
0
. We conclude that when
k+sk^{\prime}\neq 0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{D}\sin\bigg(\frac{\pi kj}{D+1}\bigg)\sin\bigg(\frac{\pi k^{\prime}j}{D+1}\bigg)=0.

We consider the case
k+sk^{\prime}=0
. By the definition, it only holds when
s=-1
and
k=k^{\prime}
. In such case, simple algebra shows that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{D}\sin\bigg(\frac{\pi kj}{D+1}\bigg)\sin\bigg(\frac{\pi k^{\prime}j}{D+1}\bigg)=\frac{D+1}{2},

which completes the proof of Lemma
D.18
.
∎

D.5
Sequence iteration bound

The following lemma is inspired by
Cao et al. (2023)
;
Meng et al. (2024)
;
Zhang et al. (2024a)
.

Lemma D.19
.

Suppose that a positive sequence
x_{t}
,
t\geq 0
follows the iterative formula

\displaystyle x_{t+1}=x_{t}+cx_{t}^{-a}

for some positive constant
a,c>0
.
Then it holds that

\displaystyle\big((a+1)ct+x_{0}^{a+1}\big)^{\frac{1}{a+1}}\leq x_{t}\leq cx_{0}^{-a}+\big((a+1)ct+x_{0}^{a+1}\big)^{\frac{1}{a+1}}

for all
t\geq 0
.

Proof of Lemma
D.19
.

We first show the lower bound of
x_{t}
. Consider a continuous-time sequence
\underline{x}_{t}
,
t\geq 0
defined by the integral equation with the same initialization.

\displaystyle\underline{x}_{t}=\underline{x}_{0}+c\cdot\int_{0}^{t}\underline{x}_{\tau}^{-a}\mathrm{d}\tau,\quad\underline{x}_{0}=x_{0}.

(D.31)

Note that
\underline{x}_{t}
is obviously an increasing function of
t
. Therefore we have

\displaystyle\underline{x}_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\underline{x}_{t}+c\cdot\int_{t}^{t+1}\underline{x}_{\tau}^{-a}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\underline{x}_{t}+c\cdot\int_{t}^{t+1}\underline{x}_{t}^{-a}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=\underline{x}_{t}+c\underline{x}_{t}^{-a}

for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
.
Comparing the above inequality with the iterative formula of
\{x_{t}\}
, we conclude by the comparison theorem that
x_{t}\geq\underline{x}_{t}
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
. Note that (
D.31
) has an exact solution

\displaystyle\underline{x}_{t}=\big((a+1)ct+x_{0}^{a+1}\big)^{\frac{1}{a+1}}

Therefore we have

\displaystyle x_{t}\geq\big((a+1)ct+x_{0}^{a+1}\big)^{\frac{1}{a+1}}

for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, which completes the first part of the proof. Now for the upper bound of
x_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle x_{t}

\displaystyle=x_{0}+c\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}x_{\tau}^{-a}

\displaystyle\leq x_{0}+c\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\big((a+1)c\tau+x_{0}^{a+1}\big)^{-\frac{a}{a+1}}

\displaystyle=x_{0}+\frac{c}{x_{0}^{a}}+c\cdot\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\big((a+1)c\tau+x_{0}^{a+1}\big)^{-\frac{a}{a+1}}

\displaystyle\leq x_{0}+\frac{c}{x_{0}^{a}}+c\cdot\int_{0}^{t}\big((a+1)c\tau+x_{0}^{a+1}\big)^{-\frac{a}{a+1}}\mathrm{d}\tau,

where the second inequality follows by the lower bound of
x_{t}
as the first part of the result of this lemma. Therefore we have

\displaystyle x_{t}

\displaystyle\leq x_{0}+cx_{0}^{-a}+\big((a+1)ct+x_{0}^{a+1}\big)^{\frac{1}{a+1}}-x_{0}

\displaystyle=cx_{0}^{-a}+\big((a+1)ct+x_{0}^{a+1}\big)^{\frac{1}{a+1}},

which completes the proof of Lemma
D.19
.
∎

Appendix E
Additional Simulation Results.

In this section, we present additional simulation results with larger numbers of variable groups
D
and higher variable dimensions
d
. Specifically, we generate data according to Definition
3.1
, setting
\sigma_{x}=0.25
and
(n,d,D)=(10000,100,100)
. We consider two scenarios with
j^{*}=30
and
j^{*}=70
, respectively, and investigate whether the attention matrix effectively concentrates on the specified
j^{*}
, even under high-dimensional settings designed to mimic the scale of image data. The vector
\mathbf{v}^{*}
is randomly generated and kept fixed throughout the simulations.

Due to the large amount of sample size
n
, we consider the SGD training with batch size
64
. We set the learning rate
\eta=0.01
and train the model for 100 epochs. During the training process, we plot training loss and the cosine similarity
\frac{\langle\mathbf{v}^{(t)}_{1},\mathbf{v}^{*}\rangle}{\|\mathbf{v}^{(t)}_{1}\|\|\mathbf{v}^{*}\|}
. After the training loss converges at the final epoch, we calculate the attention score matrix for each sample and display the heatmap of the average attention score matrix across all samples.

(a)

Training Loss

(b)

Angle in
\mathbf{v}_{1}^{(t)}
and
\mathbf{v}^{*}

(c)

Attention Matrix

(d)

Training Loss

(e)

Angle in
\mathbf{v}_{1}^{(t)}
and
\mathbf{v}^{*}

(f)

Attention Matrix

Figure 4:
Figures on training loss, cosine similarity and attention matrix. The first line presents the training results with
j^{*}=30
. The second line shows the training results for
j^{*}=70
.

As shown in Figure
4
, the training loss steadily decreases, eventually approaching zero after sufficient training iterations, and
\mathbf{v}_{1}^{(t)}
rapidly aligns with the direction of
\mathbf{v}^{*}
even when
d
and
D
have a higher dimension. More interestingly, when the value of
j^{*}
varies, the attention mechanism consistently adapts to the target index. For instance, when
j^{*}=30
, the attention matrix sharply focuses on the 30th row and effectively isolates the label-relevant group. The focus shifts with the same precision to
j^{*}=70
. This behavior highlights the model’s ability to redirect its attention to the specified index, even in high-dimensional settings designed to mimic the complexity of image data.

Appendix F
Real Data Experiments

In this section, we conduct experiments using the CIFAR-10 dataset, where each image has a shape of
3\times 32\times 32
, representing three color channels (RGB). For this experiment, we select two labels, “Frog” and “Airplane,” and use 500 images from each label. To prepare the input for our framework, each CIFAR-10 image is embedded as either the first patch (positioned at (1,1)) or the 25th patch (positioned at (4,4)) in a grid, while the remaining 48 patches are filled with noise. These noise patches, each of size
3\times 32\times 32
, are generated using random values sampled from a Gaussian distribution with a mean of
0
and a standard deviation of
1/3
. This arrangement forms a
7\times 7
grid (49 patches in total), resulting in a final input with dimensions of
3\times 224\times 224
. Examples of the processed images are shown in Figure
5
.

(a)

“Frog” Images at position (1,1)

(b)

“Airplane” Images at position (1,1)

(c)

“Frog” Images at position (4,4)

(d)

“Airplane” Images at position (4,4)

Figure 5:
Examples of embedded images. Figure
5(a)
and Figure
5(b)
show images labeled as “Frog” and “Airplane,” respectively, embedded at position (1,1) with a token index of
1
. Figure
5(c)
and Figure
5(d)
show images labeled as “Frog” and “Airplane,” respectively, embedded at position (4,4) with a token index of
25
.

(a)

Training Loss at position (1,1)

(b)

Training Loss at position (4,4)

(c)

Attention Matrix at position (1,1)

(d)

Attention Matrix at position (4,4)

(e)

Testing Accuracy at position (1,1)

(f)

Testing Accuracy at position (4,4)

Figure 6:
Experiment results on training loss, attention matrix and testing accuracy.
The first column shows the results when images have position at (1,1). The second column shows the results when images have position at (4,4).

We apply a one-layer transformer to learn from the processed images. Each patch, including the original CIFAR-10 image and the 48 noise patches, is flattened into a vector of size
3\times 32\times 32=3072
, resulting in a total of 49 vectors corresponding to the
7\times 7
grid of patches. These vectors, each with a dimension of
3072
, form the input sequence for the transformer model. As shown in Figure
5(a)
and Figure
5(b)
, images positioned at (1,1) correspond to
j^{*}=1
, while Figure
5(c)
and Figure
5(d)
show images positioned at (4,4), corresponding to
j^{*}=25
. In this setup,
d=3072
represents the dimensionality of the data, and
D=49
denotes the number of variable groups. The transformer model is initialized to
0
, and we train it using a batch size of
64
and a learning rate of
10^{-3}
. For comparison, we also present results from directly applying logistic regression to the clean data points, where each CIFAR-10 image is flattened into a single vector of size
3072
, and logistic regression is performed on these single vectors.

The results of our experiment are presented in Figures
6
. Figures
6(a)
and
6(b)
show the training loss over 100 epochs using gradient descent. The plot demonstrates a steady decrease in the loss during training, which closely aligns with the decreasing trend observed in the clean logistic regression model. This indicates the effectiveness of the optimization process and the transformer model’s ability to focus on the true images.
Figures
6(c)
and
6(d)
display the attention matrices for the trained images, further confirming the model’s ability to focus on relevant features. For images positioned at (1,1) with
j^{*}=1
, the attention matrix shows that the model focuses predominantly on the first row, corresponding to the original CIFAR-10 image. Similarly, for images positioned at (4,4) with
j^{*}=25
, the attention matrix highlights that the model concentrates on the 25th row, again corresponding to the true image.
Figures
6(e)
and
6(f)
demonstrate the generalization performance of the transformer on an unseen test dataset. The results show that the transformer maintains strong performance, further verifying its ability to effectively generalize to new data.
